\displaystyle=\left\|\mathsf{r}_{0}(\mathsf{z}+\Delta)-\mathsf{r}_{0}(\mathsf{z})\right\|_{p}=\left\|(\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes 1)[\mathsf{r}(\mathsf{z}+\Delta)\Delta\mathsf{r}(\mathsf{z})]\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathsf{r}(\mathsf{z}+\Delta)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\Delta\|_{p}\|\mathsf{r}(\mathsf{z})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\delta^{-2}\left\|\Delta\right\|_{p}

where we used
L^{p}
-contractivity of
\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes 1
(Lemma

D.3
),
Hölder’s inequality (Lemma
D.2
), the given
conditions
\Im\mathsf{z}\geq\delta
and
\Im(\mathsf{z}+\Delta)\geq\delta/2
,
and Lemma
3.6
.

Similarly for part (b), defining
\mathsf{m}_{0}(\mathsf{z})=(n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\otimes 1)[\mathsf{r}(\mathsf{z})]

and setting
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}=\mathsf{m}-\Delta
, we have

\mathsf{m}^{\prime}=n^{-1}\sum_{i}({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\Delta\mathsf{x}-\mathsf{x}\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\mathsf{x})^{-1}
so
Lemma
4.3
(a) implies that

\mathsf{m}^{\prime}=\mathsf{m}_{0}(\mathsf{z}+I\otimes\mathsf{x}\Delta\mathsf{x})
. Then, using also
\|\mathsf{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma
,

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{m}-\mathsf{m}_{0}\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta\|_{p}+\|\mathsf{m}_{0}(\mathsf{z}+I\otimes\mathsf{x}\Delta\mathsf{x})-\mathsf{m}_{0}(\mathsf{z})\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta\|_{p}+2\delta^{-2}\|I\otimes\mathsf{x}\Delta\mathsf{x}\|_{p}\leq(1+2\gamma^{2}\delta^{-2})\|\Delta\|_{p}.

∎

We close this section with an analysis of the quantitative invertibility of two
linear operators
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
in the
L^{p}
-norms, defined as

\mathcal{L}_{1}(\mathsf{a})=\mathsf{a}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii},\qquad\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{a})=\mathsf{a}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}.

The invertibility of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
is more immediate, and follows from
differentiating the preceding fixed-point equation.
We state this in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.5
.

Under Assumption
4.1
, consider the linear operator

\mathcal{L}_{1}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
given by

\mathcal{L}_{1}(\mathsf{a})=\mathsf{a}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}.

Then
\mathcal{L}_{1}
is invertible, and for any
\mathsf{a}\in\mathcal{X}
and
p\in[1,\infty]

(where
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}=\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}}
),

\|\mathcal{L}_{1}^{-1}(\mathsf{a})\|_{p}\leq\gamma^{2}(\gamma^{2}+\delta^{-2})\|\mathsf{a}\|_{p}.

Proof.

For any
\mathsf{b}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\|\mathsf{b}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\delta/2

(which ensures
\Im(\mathsf{z}+1\otimes\mathsf{b})>\delta/2>0
), define

f(\mathsf{b})=(\tau\otimes 1)[(\mathsf{h}\otimes\mathsf{x}-\mathsf{z}-1\otimes\mathsf{b})^{-1}],\qquad\omega(\mathsf{b})=\mathsf{x}^{-1}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{x}^{-1}+f(\mathsf{b}).

Then Lemma
4.3
(b) applied at
\mathsf{z}+1\otimes\mathsf{b}
shows
that
f(\mathsf{b})
is the unique solution in
\mathcal{X}^{+}
to the fixed-point equation

f(\mathsf{b})=n^{-1}\sum_{i}({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{b}-\mathsf{x}f(\mathsf{b})\mathsf{x})^{-1}
, i.e.

(55)

\mathsf{x}^{-1}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{x}^{-1}=\omega(\mathsf{b})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\,\omega(\mathsf{b})\mathsf{x})^{-1}.

For all
\omega\in\mathcal{X}^{+}
, define

(56)

g(\omega)=\omega-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\,\omega\mathsf{x})^{-1}.

Then for all
\mathsf{b}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\|\mathsf{b}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\delta/2
, since

\omega(\mathsf{b})
satisfies (
55
), we have

g(\omega(\mathsf{b}))=\mathsf{x}^{-1}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{x}^{-1}
.

Let us write
D\omega(\mathsf{b}),Dg(\omega)
for the Fréchet derivatives of

\omega(\mathsf{b})
and
g(\omega)
as linear maps on
\mathcal{X}
.
Recalling
\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{h}\otimes\mathsf{x}-\mathsf{z})^{-1}
and
differentiating
\omega(\mathsf{b})=\mathsf{x}^{-1}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{x}^{-1}+(\tau\otimes 1)[(\mathsf{h}\otimes\mathsf{x}-\mathsf{z}-1\otimes\mathsf{b})^{-1}]
at
\mathsf{b}=0
, for any
\mathsf{a}\in\mathcal{X}
and
p\in[1,\infty]
we have

\displaystyle\|D\omega(0)[\mathsf{a}]\|_{p}

\displaystyle=\|\mathsf{x}^{-1}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}^{-1}+(\tau\otimes 1)[\mathsf{r}(1\otimes\mathsf{a})\mathsf{r}]\|_{p}

(57)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2}\|\mathsf{a}\|_{p}+\|\mathsf{r}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|1\otimes\mathsf{a}\|_{p}\leq(\gamma^{2}+\delta^{-2})\|\mathsf{a}\|_{p}.

Here, the second line uses Hölder’s inequality (Lemma
D.2
),
\|\mathsf{x}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma
,
contractivity of the conditional expectation
\tau\otimes 1
in
L^{p}

(Lemma
D.3
),
and
\|\mathsf{r}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
by the assumption
\Im\mathsf{z}\geq\delta
and
Lemma
3.6
.
On the other hand, differentiating (
56
)
at
\omega_{0}=\omega(0)
and using
({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\omega_{0}\mathsf{x})^{-1}=({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\mathsf{m}_{0}\mathsf{x})^{-1}=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}

by Lemma
4.3
(a), we have

Dg(\omega_{0})[\mathsf{a}]=\mathsf{a}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}=\mathcal{L}_{1}(\mathsf{a}).

Then differentiating both sides of the identity

g(\omega(\mathsf{b}))=\mathsf{x}^{-1}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{x}^{-1}
at
\mathsf{b}=0
and evaluating the derivative at

\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}
for any
\mathsf{a}\in\mathcal{X}
gives

\mathcal{L}_{1}(D\omega(0)[\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}])=Dg(\omega_{0})[D\omega(0)[\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}]]=\mathsf{x}^{-1}[\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}]\mathsf{x}^{-1}=\mathsf{a}.

The bound (
57
) for
p=\infty
shows that

\mathsf{a}\mapsto D\omega(0)[\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}]
defines a bounded linear operator on

\mathcal{X}
, so
\mathcal{L}_{1}
is invertible with inverse given explicitly by

\mathcal{L}_{1}^{-1}(\mathsf{a})=D\omega(0)[\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}]
. Finally, (
57
)
and the condition
\|\mathsf{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma

imply that
\|\mathcal{L}_{1}^{-1}(\mathsf{a})\|_{p}\leq\gamma^{2}(\gamma^{2}+\delta^{-2})\|\mathsf{a}\|_{p}
.
∎

We now turn to the invertibility of
\mathcal{L}_{2}
. We use an idea from

[
2
, Section 4.2]
, which relies on the observation
that
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}
may be controlled at a single positive element of
\mathcal{X}

by taking imaginary parts of (
52
), and that
the linear map
\mathsf{a}\mapsto(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}
is
positivity-preserving. For finite-dimensional matrix algebras, this implies
that this map has a Perron-Frobenius eigenvector
\mathsf{a}\geq 0
in the
positive cone. The analyses of
[
2
]
construct a
symmetrized version of this map that is self-adjoint, so that its
L^{2}\to L^{2}

operator norm coincides with its spectral radius, and then apply a

L^{2}
-inner-product of the Perron-Frobenius eigenvector with the imaginary part
of (
52
) to deduce a quantitative bound on

\|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}
.

We adapt this idea to address two additional challenges in our setting: First,
as the map
\mathsf{a}\mapsto(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}
is non-compact and may
have continuous spectrum, we can only guarantee the existence of an approximate
Perron-Frobenius eigenvector (c.f. Lemma
4.6

below). For reasons that will be clear in the proof, the approximation error
must be controlled in
L^{1}
rather than
L^{2}
, and thus we implement a version
of this argument in the
L^{1}
-
L^{\infty}
duality rather than in a Hilbert space
setting, to obtain a bound for
\|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}}
.
Second, as we will also require a bound on
\|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{p}\to L^{p}}
for
each
p\in[1,\infty)
, we carry out a dual version of this argument
to obtain also a bound for
\|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{1}\to L^{1}}
, and hence
deduce a bound on
\|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{p}\to L^{p}}
via the Riesz-Thorin
interpolation.

Lemma 4.6

(Approximate Perron-Frobenius
eigenvector)
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\|\cdot\|)
be a real Banach space, and
\mathcal{K}\subset\mathcal{X}
a
closed convex cone such that
\mathcal{K}\cap(-\mathcal{K})=\{0\}
(
\mathcal{K}
is proper),

\mathcal{X}=\{x-y:x,y\in\mathcal{K}\}
(
\mathcal{K}
is generating), and for some
C>0
we have

\|x\|\leq C\|x+y\|
whenever
x,y\in\mathcal{K}
(
\mathcal{K}
is normal).

Let
T:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
be a bounded linear operator such that
T(\mathcal{K})\subseteq\mathcal{K}
, and let
r(T)
be the spectral radius of its complexification

T_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathcal{X}+i\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}+i\mathcal{X}
. Then
r(T)
is an element of the spectrum of
T
.
Furthermore, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists an approximate eigenvector

x
of
T
such that

x\in\mathcal{K},\qquad\|x\|=1,\qquad\|T(x)-r(T)x\|<\epsilon.

Proof.

See
[
34
, Lemma 3.5]
.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

Under Assumption
4.1
, consider the linear operator

\mathcal{L}_{2}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
given by

\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{a})=\mathsf{a}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}.

Then
\mathcal{L}_{2}
is invertible, and for any
\mathsf{a}\in\mathcal{X}
and
p\in[1,\infty]

(where
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}=\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}}
),

(58)

\|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}(\mathsf{a})\|_{p}\leq\frac{2\gamma^{4}(\upsilon+\gamma^{2}\delta^{-1})^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\|\mathsf{a}\|_{p}.

Proof.

Denote
L^{p}\equiv L^{p}(\mathcal{X})
for the non-commutative
L^{p}
-spaces associated to

\mathcal{X}
(Appendix
D
)
and define
\mathcal{F}:L^{1}\to L^{1}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}:L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}
by

\mathcal{F}(\mathsf{b})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}\mathsf{b}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x},\qquad\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\mathsf{a})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}.

Here
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
are bounded linear operators on the Banach spaces

(L^{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})
and
(L^{\infty},\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}})
,
by Hölder’s inequality and the bounds
\|(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\mathsf{r}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
and
\|\mathsf{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma
.
Identifying the dual
(L^{1})^{*}
with
L^{\infty}
via the isometry

\mathsf{a}\in L^{\infty}\mapsto\ell_{\mathsf{a}}\in(L^{1})^{*}
where
\ell_{\mathsf{a}}(\mathsf{b})=\phi(\mathsf{b}\mathsf{a})

(Lemma
D.2
), for any
\mathsf{a}\in L^{\infty}
and
\mathsf{b}\in L^{1}

we have
\ell_{\mathsf{a}}(\mathcal{F}(\mathsf{b}))=\phi(\mathcal{F}(\mathsf{b})\mathsf{a})=\phi(b\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\mathsf{a}))=\ell_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\mathsf{a})}(\mathsf{b})
, so
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is the Banach space adjoint of
\mathcal{F}
.

Let
r(\mathcal{F})
be the spectral radius of
\mathcal{F}
as an operator on
L^{1}
. Note that

\mathcal{F}
restricts to a bounded linear operator on the real Banach space of
self-adjoint elements
L^{1}_{\text{sa}}=\{\mathsf{a}\in L^{1}:\mathsf{a}=\mathsf{a}^{*}\}
(whose
complexification is
\mathcal{F}
itself), which furthermore
preserves the positive cone
L^{1}_{\text{pos}}=\{\mathsf{a}\in L^{1}_{\text{sa}}:\mathsf{a}\geq 0\}
.
Any
\mathsf{a}\in L^{1}_{\text{sa}}
may be decomposed as
\mathsf{a}=\mathsf{a}_{+}-\mathsf{a}_{-}
with
\mathsf{a}_{+},\mathsf{a}_{-}\in L^{1}_{\text{pos}}
via
\mathsf{a}_{+}=(|\mathsf{a}|+\mathsf{a})/2
and
\mathsf{a}_{-}=(|\mathsf{a}|-\mathsf{a})/2
,
and we have
\|\mathsf{a}\|_{1}=\tau(\mathsf{a})\leq\tau(\mathsf{a}+\mathsf{b})=\|\mathsf{a}+\mathsf{b}\|_{1}
for all

\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}\in L^{1}_{\text{pos}}
by positivity of the trace.
Thus
L^{1}_{\text{pos}}
is a proper, generating, and
normal cone in
L^{1}_{\text{sa}}
, so Lemma
4.6

ensures the existence of some
\mathsf{v}\in L^{1}
satisfying

(59)

\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{v}^{*},\qquad\mathsf{v}\geq 0,\qquad\|\mathsf{v}\|_{1}=1,\qquad\mathcal{F}(\mathsf{v})=r(\mathcal{F})\mathsf{v}+\Delta\text{ where }\|\Delta\|_{1}<\epsilon.

Lemma
4.3
shows that
\mathsf{m}_{0}
satisfies the
fixed-point equation
\mathsf{m}_{0}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\mathsf{m}_{0}\mathsf{x})^{-1}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}
. Taking imaginary parts,
this gives
\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}(\Im\mathsf{z}_{i}+\mathsf{x}[\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}]\mathsf{x})(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}
,
which may be rearranged as

(60)

\mathcal{L}_{2}[\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}]=(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{F}^{\prime})[\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}]=\mathsf{u},\qquad\text{ for }\mathsf{u}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}[\Im\mathsf{z}_{i}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}.

Multiplying on the left by the above approximate eigenvector
\mathsf{v}
and taking
the trace,

\phi(\mathsf{v}\mathsf{u})=\phi(\mathsf{v}\Im\mathsf{m}_{0})-\phi(\mathsf{v}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}[\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}])=\phi(\mathsf{v}\Im\mathsf{m}_{0})-\phi(\mathcal{F}[\mathsf{v}]\Im\mathsf{m}_{0})=(1-r(\mathcal{F}))\phi(\mathsf{v}\Im\mathsf{m}_{0})-\phi(\Delta\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}).

Thus

1-r(\mathcal{F})=\frac{\phi(\mathsf{v}\mathsf{u})+\phi(\Delta\Im\mathsf{m}_{0})}{\phi(\mathsf{v}\Im\mathsf{m}_{0})}.

By Hölder’s inequality and the bound
\|\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\mathsf{m}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
, we have

(61)

0\leq\phi(\mathsf{v}\Im\mathsf{m}_{0})\leq\delta^{-1},\qquad|\phi(\Delta\Im\mathsf{m}_{0})|\leq\delta^{-1}\epsilon.

We have also
\Im\mathsf{z}_{i}\geq\delta

and
\|(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|\mathsf{z}_{i}+\mathsf{x}\mathsf{m}_{0}\mathsf{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\mathsf{z}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\mathsf{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|\mathsf{m}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\upsilon+\gamma^{2}\delta^{-1}
,
implying that

(62)

\mathsf{u}\geq\delta\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}\geq c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)\qquad\text{ for }c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)=\delta(\upsilon+\gamma^{2}\delta^{-1})^{-2}.

Then by positivity of
\mathsf{v}
, positivity of the trace, and the normalization

\|\mathsf{v}\|_{1}=1
, we have

\phi(\mathsf{v}\mathsf{u})=\phi(\mathsf{v}^{1/2}\mathsf{u}\mathsf{v}^{1/2})\geq c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)\phi(\mathsf{v})=c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)
.
Applying these bounds above and then taking
\epsilon\to 0
, we obtain

(63)

1-r(\mathcal{F})\geq\delta c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)>0.

Since
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is the adjoint of
\mathcal{F}
and thus shares its spectrum,
this shows
r(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=r(\mathcal{F})<1
. Then

\mathcal{L}_{2}=\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is invertible as a bounded linear operator on
L^{\infty}
, and
its inverse has the Neumann series representation

\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{k}

which is convergent in the induced operator norm
\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}}
(by Gelfand’s formula
r(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=\lim_{k\to\infty}\|{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}}^{1/k}
). Thus, for
any
\mathsf{a}\in L^{\infty}
, we have
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}(\mathsf{a})=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{k}[\mathsf{a}]

which is convergent under
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. We note that
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{k}
is also
positivity-preserving, i.e. if
\mathsf{a}\geq 0
, then
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{k}[\mathsf{a}]\geq 0
.
Thus if
\mathsf{a}\geq 0
, then
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}(\mathsf{a})\geq 0
since
the positive cone is closed under
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. To summarize, we have
shown that
\mathcal{L}_{2}:L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}
is invertible, and

(64)

\mathsf{a}\geq 0\Longrightarrow\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}(\mathsf{a})\geq 0.

Now take any
\mathsf{b}\in L^{\infty}
self-adjoint.
Applying (
62
), we have

\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{b})\leq\|\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{b})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot 1_{\mathcal{X}}\leq\|\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{b})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{u}

so the monotonicity of
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}
in (
64
) and explicit form
of
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}[\mathsf{u}]
in (
60
) imply

\mathsf{b}\leq\|\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{b})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}[\mathsf{u}]=\|\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{b})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)^{-1}\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}.

Similarly
\mathsf{b}\geq{-}\|\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{b})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot c(K,\upsilon,\delta)^{-1}\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}
.
Applying again
\|\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
, this shows
for every
\mathsf{b}\in L^{\infty}
self-adjoint that

\|\mathsf{b}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}c(K,\upsilon,\delta)^{-1}\|\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{b})\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.
Then for any (non-self-adjoint)
\mathsf{a}\in L^{\infty}
,
noting that
(\|\Re\mathsf{a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\Im\mathsf{a}\|_{\mathrm{op}})/2\leq\|\mathsf{a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\Re\mathsf{a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\Im\mathsf{a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
and that
\Re\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{a})=\mathcal{L}_{2}(\Re\mathsf{a})
and
\Im\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{a})=\mathcal{L}_{2}(\Im\mathsf{a})
, this implies

(65)

\|\mathsf{a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\delta^{-1}c(\gamma,\delta,\upsilon)^{-1}\|\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{a})\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\frac{2(\upsilon+\gamma^{2}\delta^{-1})^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\|\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{a})\|_{\mathrm{op}},

which implies (
58
) for
p=\infty
.

Next, we show (
58
) for
p=1
using a dual argument: As
L^{1}

is not reflexive, we reverse the roles of

\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and define the bounded linear operators
\mathcal{G}:L^{1}\to L^{1}

and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}:L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}
by

\mathcal{G}(\mathsf{b})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*},\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(\mathsf{a})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}\mathsf{a}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}.

Then again
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is the adjoint of
\mathcal{G}
. For any
\epsilon>0
, Lemma

4.6
shows there exists
\mathsf{v}\in L^{1}
satisfying

\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{v}^{*},\qquad\mathsf{v}\geq 0,\qquad\|\mathsf{v}\|_{1}=1,\qquad\mathcal{G}(\mathsf{v})=r(\mathcal{G})\mathsf{v}+\Delta\text{ where }\|\Delta\|_{1}<\epsilon.

Now taking imaginary parts of the fixed-point equation

\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}^{*}-\mathsf{x}\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*}\mathsf{x})^{-1}
, we have

\Im(\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*})=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}(\Im(\mathsf{z}_{i}^{*})+\mathsf{x}[\Im(\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*})]\mathsf{x})(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}
.
Negating and applying
\Im(\mathsf{a}^{*})={-}\Im\mathsf{a}
, this gives

\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}=\mathsf{x}^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(\mathsf{x}[\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}]\mathsf{x})\mathsf{x}^{-1}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}[\Im\mathsf{z}_{i}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}.

Thus, in place of (
60
) we have

(66)

(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{G}^{\prime})[\mathsf{x}(\Im\mathsf{m}_{0})\mathsf{x}]=\mathsf{w},\qquad\text{ for }\mathsf{w}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}^{*}[\Im\mathsf{z}_{i}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}.

In place of (
61
) and (
62
), we may apply

\phi(\mathsf{v}\mathsf{x}[\Im\mathsf{m}_{0}]\mathsf{x})\leq\gamma^{2}\delta^{-1}
and

\mathsf{w}\geq\gamma^{-2}c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)
,
where the first inequality uses
\|\mathsf{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma
and the
second uses
\|\mathsf{x}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma
and

c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)
as defined in (
62
).
Then, multiplying (
66
) by
\mathsf{v}
, taking the trace, and then
taking the limit
\epsilon\to 0
, we obtain similarly to (
63
)
that
1-r(\mathcal{G})\geq\delta\gamma^{-4}c(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)>0
.
This implies that
\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{G}^{\prime}:L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}

is invertible with positivity-preserving inverse,
and repeating the preceding arguments gives, for any
\mathsf{a}\in L^{\infty}
,

\|\mathsf{a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{2\gamma^{4}(\upsilon+\gamma^{2}\delta^{-1})^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{G}^{\prime})[\mathsf{a}]\|_{\mathrm{op}},

Since
(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{G}^{\prime})^{-1}
is the adjoint of
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}=(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{G})^{-1}:L^{1}\to L^{1}
, we have

\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{G}^{\prime})^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}}=\|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{1}\to L^{1}}
.
Thus, this shows also for any
\mathsf{a}\in L^{1}
that

(67)

\|\mathsf{a}\|_{L^{1}}\leq\frac{2\gamma^{4}(\upsilon+\gamma^{2}\delta^{-1})^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\|\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathsf{a})\|_{L^{1}},

which is the desired result (
58
) for
p=1
.

Finally, the result (
58
) for general
p\in[1,\infty]
follows
from the bounds for
\|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}}
and

\|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}\|_{L^{1}\to L^{1}}
already shown, and the Riesz-Thorin interpolation
(Lemma
D.4
).
∎

4.2.
Weak estimates in operator norm

Lemma 4.8
.

Under Assumption
4.1
, there exists a constant
\alpha\in(0,1/2)

depending only on
\gamma,\upsilon,\delta
such that for any
l\geq 1
,
D>0
,
and all
n\geq n_{0}(l,K,\gamma,\upsilon,\delta,D)
,
with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
,

(68)

\displaystyle\sup_{S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}:|S|\leq l}\;\sup_{i\notin S}\left\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}\left(|h_{ir}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)R_{rr}^{(iS)}+\sum_{r\neq s}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rs}^{(iS)}h_{si}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}<n^{-\alpha},

(69)

\displaystyle\sup_{S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}:|S|\leq l}\;\sup_{i,j\notin S:i\neq j}\left\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}<n^{-\alpha}.

Proof.

We present the argument for (
68
):
Take any
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l
, and any
i\notin S
.
Let
H^{(iS)}
be as defined in Section
3.4
,
and let
\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
be the
i^{\text{th}}
column of
H^{(iS)}
, i.e. the vector with entries
(\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)})_{j}=h_{ji}

for
j\notin S\cup\{i\}
and 0 otherwise.
Let
(\mathcal{H},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{H}})
be the Hilbert space on
which
\mathcal{X}
acts. For any
unit vectors
\xi,\zeta\in\mathcal{H}
, define the linear functional

f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}:\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}\otimes\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{C}
by

\displaystyle f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}(M)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\xi,\,\left(\sum_{r}^{(iS)}\left(|h_{ir}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)M_{rr}+\sum_{r\neq s}^{(iS)}h_{ir}M_{rs}h_{si}\right)\zeta\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\xi,\,\left((\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\otimes 1)^{*}M(\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\otimes 1)-(n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}^{(iS)}\otimes 1)M\right)\zeta\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\quad\text{ for }\operatorname{Tr}^{(iS)}M:=\sum_{j}^{(iS)}M_{jj}

where
M_{rs}=(\mathbf{e}_{r}\otimes 1)^{*}M(\mathbf{e}_{s}\otimes 1)
is the
\mathcal{X}
-valued
(r,s)

entry of
M
. Define

M^{(iS)}:\mathbb{C}^{+}\to\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}\otimes\mathcal{X}
by

M^{(iS)}(z)=\left(H^{(iS)}\otimes\mathsf{x}-\mathsf{z}+i(\delta/2)-z\right)^{-1}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}\otimes\mathcal{X}.

Note that
\Im(\mathsf{z}-i(\delta/2)+z)>\delta/2
for all
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, so by Lemma

3.6
, this inverse is well-defined and

(70)

\|M^{(iS)}(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2/\delta\text{ for all }z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}.

By definition we have
R^{(iS)}=M^{(iS)}(i\delta/2)
, so
the operator norm to be bounded in (
68
) is

\left\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}\left(|h_{ir}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)R_{rr}^{(iS)}+\sum_{r\neq s}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rs}^{(iS)}h_{si}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\sup_{\xi,\zeta\in\mathcal{H}:\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\|\zeta\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=1}\,f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(i\delta/2).

Set
C_{0}=3\gamma+(3/2)\upsilon
,
\mathcal{D}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}:|z|\geq 2C_{0}\}
, and

\epsilon=0.1
, and define the event

\mathcal{E}=\bigcap_{S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}:|S|\leq l}\;\bigcap_{i\notin S}\;\bigcap_{\xi,\zeta:\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\|\zeta\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=1}\left\{\sup_{z\in\mathcal{D}}|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(z)|\leq n^{-1/2+\epsilon}\right\}\cap\{\|\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\|_{2}\leq 3\}.

Noting that
f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(z)
is an analytic function of

z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
,
we apply Lemma
3.8
on this event
\mathcal{E}
: Let
a=3C_{0}
,
and set
r_{0}<r_{1}<r_{2}<0
such that

a\frac{1-e^{r_{0}}}{1+e^{r_{0}}}=2C_{0},\qquad a\frac{1-e^{r_{1}}}{1+e^{r_{1}}}=\delta/2,\qquad a\frac{1-e^{r_{2}}}{1+e^{r_{2}}}=\delta/4.

Then defining
S_{r}
as in Lemma
3.8
, we have

S_{r_{0}}\subset\mathcal{D}
and
i\delta/2\in S_{r_{1}}
. On
\mathcal{E}
, the
inclusion
S_{r_{0}}\subset\mathcal{D}
implies

|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(z)|\leq n^{-1/2+\epsilon}
for all
z\in S_{r_{0}}
,
and the bounds (
70
) and
\|\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\|_{2}\leq 3
imply

|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(z)|\leq 20/\delta
for
all
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
. Thus Lemma
3.8
shows

\displaystyle\log|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(i\delta/2)|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{z\in S_{r_{1}}}\log|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|r_{1}-r_{2}|}{|r_{2}-r_{0}|}\sup_{z\in S_{r_{0}}}\log|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(z)|+\frac{|r_{1}-r_{0}|}{|r_{2}-r_{0}|}\sup_{z\in S_{r_{2}}}\log|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq-\alpha\log n

for some constant
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
depending only on

\gamma,\upsilon,\delta
, and for all
n\geq n_{0}(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)
. Thus
(
68
) holds on the event
\mathcal{E}
for all such
n
.

We now check that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}]\geq 1-n^{-D}
for all
n\geq n_{0}(l,K,D)
:
By a standard tail bound for the operator norm
(see e.g.
[
24
, Theorem 7.3]
), the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0}

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}:|S|\leq l}\;\bigcap_{i\notin S}\left\{\|H^{(iS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\right\}\subset\{\|H\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\}

holds with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
for all
n\geq n_{0}(D)
. On

\mathcal{E}_{0}
, we have also
\|\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\|_{2}\leq 3
.
Set for notational convenience
D_{0}={-}H^{(iS)}

(which depends implicitly on
i
and
S
),
\mathsf{x}_{0}=\mathsf{x}
,
D_{K+1}={-}i\delta/2
,
and
\mathsf{x}_{K+1}=1
, so that

H^{(iS)}\otimes\mathsf{x}-\mathsf{z}+i(\delta/2)={-}\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}D_{k}\otimes\mathsf{x}_{k}.

By the assumptions (
43
), we have on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
that

\left\|H^{(iS)}\otimes\mathsf{x}-\mathsf{z}+i(\delta/2)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}\|D_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathsf{x}_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\gamma+(3/2)\upsilon=C_{0}.

Then, since
|z|\geq 2C_{0}
for
z\in\mathcal{D}
, the series
expansion of
M^{(iS)}(z)
in
z^{-1}
is absolutely convergent in operator
norm, and

(71)

M^{(iS)}(z)={-}\sum_{t=0}^{T(n)}z^{-(t+1)}\left({-}\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}D_{k}\otimes\mathsf{x}_{k}\right)^{t}+J^{(iS)}(z),\qquad\|J^{(iS)}(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n^{-1}

for some
T(n)\leq C\log n
and an absolute constant
C>0
.
On
\mathcal{E}_{0}
, using again
\|\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\|_{2}\leq 3
,
we have
|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}(M)|\leq 10\|M\|_{\mathrm{op}}
for any
M\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}\otimes\mathcal{X}
.
Thus, for the remainder of (
71
),

(72)

|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ J^{(iS)}(z)|\leq 10\|J^{(iS)}(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 10n^{-1}.

For the leading terms of (
71
), let us expand

(73)

\left(\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}D_{k}\otimes\mathsf{x}_{k}\right)^{t}=\sum_{w\in\mathcal{W}_{t}}\underbrace{w(D_{0},\ldots,D_{K+1})}_{:=w(D^{(iS)})}\otimes\underbrace{w(\mathsf{x}_{0},\ldots,\mathsf{x}_{K+1})}_{:=w(\mathsf{x})}

where
\mathcal{W}_{t}
denotes the set of all length-
t
words (i.e. non-commutative
degree-
t
monic monomials) in
K+2
variables. For
t=0
, we use the convention

\mathcal{W}_{t}=\{o\}
where
o
is the word of length 0, with
o(D^{(iS)})=I
and

o(\mathsf{x})=1
. Then for each
w\in\mathcal{W}_{t}
,
by the definition of
f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}
,
we have the factorization

(74)

\displaystyle f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\big(w(D^{(iS)})\otimes w(\mathsf{x})\big)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)*}w(D^{(iS)})\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}-n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}^{(iS)}w(D^{(iS)})\right)\cdot\langle\xi,w(\mathsf{x})\zeta\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

Since
\mathbb{E}[|h_{ji}|^{2}]=n^{-1}
,
the scalar version of Lemma
3.4
(a,c) (i.e. with

\mathcal{X}=\mathbb{C}
) implies that
uniformly over deterministic matrices
M\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
,

(75)

\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)*}M\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}-n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}^{(iS)}M\prec n^{-1}\left(\sum_{r,s}^{(iS)}|M_{rs}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\prec n^{-1/2}\|M\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Note that
w(D^{(iS)})
is independent of
\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}
, and

|\{S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}:|S|\leq l\}|\leq n^{l}

and
|\mathcal{W}_{t}|=(K+2)^{t}\leq n^{C\log(K+2)}
for all
t\leq T(n)\leq C\log n
.
Then, fixing
\epsilon^{\prime}=0.05
, applying (
75
)
to each matrix
M=w(D^{(iS)})
conditional on
H^{(iS)}
,
and taking a union bound, the event

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\bigcap_{S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}:|S|\leq l}\;\bigcap_{i\notin S}\bigcap_{t=0}^{T(n)}\bigcap_{w\in\mathcal{W}_{t}}\left\{\left|\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)*}w(D^{(iS)})\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}-n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}^{(iS)}w(D^{(iS)})\right|\leq n^{-1/2+\epsilon^{\prime}}\|w(D^{(iS)})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\}

holds with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
for all
n\geq n_{0}(l,K,D)
.
On
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, applying (
74
) to (
73
)
gives, for each
t=0,\ldots,T(n)
,

\displaystyle\left|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\left(\left(\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}D_{k}\otimes\mathsf{x}_{k}\right)^{t}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1/2+\epsilon^{\prime}}\sum_{w\in\mathcal{W}_{t}}\|w(D^{(iS)})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|w(\mathsf{x})\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1/2+\epsilon^{\prime}}\sum_{w\in\mathcal{W}_{t}}w(\|D_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathsf{x}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\ldots,\|D_{K+1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathsf{x}_{K+1}\|_{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle=n^{-1/2+\epsilon^{\prime}}\Big(\|D_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathsf{x}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\ldots+\|D_{K+1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathsf{x}_{K+1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\Big)^{t}

(76)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1/2+\epsilon^{\prime}}C_{0}^{t}.

Then, applying (
76
) and (
72
)
to (
71
), we have on
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}
for all
z\in\mathcal{D}
that

|f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}\circ M^{(iS)}(z)|\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T(n)}|z|^{-(t+1)}n^{-1/2+\epsilon^{\prime}}C_{0}^{t}+10n^{-1}\leq n^{-1/2+\epsilon},

the final inequality holding for our preceding choices of
\epsilon=0.1
,

\epsilon^{\prime}=0.05
, and all
n\geq n_{0}
since
|z|\geq 2C_{0}
.
So
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
, implying that

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}]\geq 1-n^{-D}
for
n\geq n_{0}(l,K,D)
, as claimed.

This shows that (
68
) holds with probability
1-n^{-D}
.
The proof for (
69
) is the same, applying these arguments with
the function

f_{i,j,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}(M)=\left\langle\xi,\left(\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}M_{rj}\right)\zeta\right\rangle=\left\langle\xi,\left((\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\otimes 1)^{*}M(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes 1)\right)\zeta\right\rangle

in place of
f_{i,\xi,\zeta}^{(S)}
.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

Under Assumption
4.1
, there exists a constant

\alpha\in(0,1/2)
depending only on
\gamma,\upsilon,\delta

such that for any
l\geq 1
,
D>0
, and all

n\geq n_{0}(l,K,\gamma,\upsilon,\delta,D)
, with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
,

(77)

\sup_{S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}:|S|\leq l}\;\sup_{i\notin S}\|R_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<n^{-\alpha},\qquad\sup_{S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}:|S|\leq l}\;\sup_{i,j\notin S:i\neq j}\|R_{ij}^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<n^{-\alpha}.

Proof.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
be as in
Lemma
4.8
. Fixing
l\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{E}
be
the event on which the statements (
68
–
69
) of
Lemma
4.8
hold, and in addition,

\sup_{i=1}^{n}|h_{ii}|<n^{-\alpha}
. Lemma
4.8

and Assumption
2.1
for
H
imply that

\mathcal{E}
holds with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
for all
n\geq n_{0}(l,K,\gamma,\upsilon,\delta,D)
.

Take any
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l
, and any
i,j\notin S

with
i\neq j
. By Lemma
3.7
(b), on
\mathcal{E}
,

(78)

\|R_{ij}^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|R_{ii}^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathsf{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma\delta^{-1}n^{-\alpha}

where we have used also
\|R_{ii}^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|R^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
by Lemma
3.6
and the assumption
\|\mathsf{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma
. Adjusting the
value of
\alpha
yields the second statement of (
77
).

For the first statement of (
77
),
take any
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l
. Define

\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}=R_{ii}^{(S)}
for
i\notin S
, and

(79)

\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}=\bigg({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}^{(S)}R_{jj}^{(S)}\bigg]\mathsf{x}\bigg)^{-1}\text{ for }i\in S.

By this definition and Lemma
3.7
(a), for each

i=1,\ldots,n
we have

(80)

\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}=\bigg({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\Delta_{i}^{(S)}-\mathsf{x}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{R}_{jj}^{(S)}\bigg]\mathsf{x}\bigg)^{-1}

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}^{(S)}

\displaystyle={-}h_{ii}\mathsf{x}+\mathsf{x}\left(\sum_{r,s}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rs}^{(iS)}h_{si}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{R}_{jj}^{(S)}\right)\mathsf{x}

\displaystyle={-}h_{ii}\mathsf{x}+\mathsf{x}\Bigg[\underbrace{\sum_{r}^{(iS)}\left(|h_{ir}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)R_{rr}^{(iS)}+\sum_{r\neq s}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rs}^{(iS)}h_{si}}_{:=\mathrm{I}}-\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in S\cup\{i\}}\widetilde{R}_{jj}^{(S)}}_{:=\mathrm{II}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}^{(iS)}(R_{jj}^{(iS)}-R_{jj}^{(S)})}_{:=\mathrm{III}}\Bigg]\mathsf{x}

(81)

\displaystyle\text{ for all }i\notin S

and

(82)

\Delta_{i}^{(S)}={-}\mathsf{x}\bigg[\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in S}\widetilde{R}_{jj}^{(S)}}_{:=\mathrm{IV}}\bigg]\mathsf{x}\qquad\text{ for all }i\in S.

On
\mathcal{E}
, we have
\|\mathrm{I}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n^{-\alpha}
by (
68
).
For
\mathrm{II}
and
\mathrm{IV}
, Lemma
3.6
implies

\|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
for both
i\in S
and
i\notin S
, the latter because
\|R_{ii}^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|R^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
and the former because
\Im R^{(S)}\geq 0
so

\Im R_{jj}^{(S)}\geq 0
and
\Im\mathsf{z}_{i}\geq\delta
in the definition (
79
). Then

(83)

\|\mathrm{II}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\mathrm{IV}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{(|S|+1)\delta^{-1}}{n}.

For
\mathrm{III}
, we have by Lemma
3.7
(b–c) that

(84)

\mathrm{III}={-}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}^{(iS)}R_{ji}^{(S)}\frac{1}{R_{ii}^{(S)}}R_{ij}^{(S)}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}^{(iS)}R_{ji}^{(S)}\mathsf{x}\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}.

Thus on
\mathcal{E}
,
\|\mathrm{III}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma^{2}\delta^{-1}n^{-2\alpha}

by (
69
) and (
78
).
Collecting these bounds and applying also
|h_{ii}|\leq n^{-\alpha}
on
\mathcal{E}
,
we have for a constant
C(\gamma,\delta)>0
and every
i=1,\ldots,n
that

(85)

\|\Delta_{i}^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C(\gamma,\delta)n^{-\alpha}\text{ on }\mathcal{E}.

Taking the Kronecker product with
E_{ii}
on both sides of
(
80
), summing over
i=1,\ldots,n
,
recalling
\mathsf{z}=\sum_{i}E_{ii}\otimes\mathsf{z}_{i}
, and setting

\Delta^{(S)}=\sum_{i}E_{ii}\otimes\Delta_{i}^{(S)}
, we have

(86)

\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ii}\otimes\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}=\left(-\mathsf{z}-\Delta^{(S)}-I_{n\times n}\otimes\mathsf{x}\left(\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}\otimes 1\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ii}\otimes\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}\right]\right)\mathsf{x}\right)^{-1}.

On
\mathcal{E}
, by the estimate
\|\Delta^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\max_{i}\|\Delta_{i}^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C(\gamma,\delta)n^{-\alpha}
from (
85
),
for all
n\geq n_{0}(\gamma,\upsilon,\delta)
this
implies
\Im(\mathsf{z}+\Delta^{(S)})\geq\delta/2
, so Corollary

4.4
(a) shows

(87)

\max_{i=1}^{n}\|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ii}\otimes\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-\mathsf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\delta^{-2}\|\Delta^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\delta^{-1}C(\gamma,\delta)n^{-\alpha}.

Then specializing this to
i\notin S
and
adjusting the value of
\alpha

yields the first statement of (
77
).
∎

4.3.
Iterative bootstrapping

We now improve the preceding estimates of Lemma
4.9

in the
L^{p}
-norms for
p<\infty
, using the following bootstrapping lemma.

Lemma 4.10
.

Suppose Assumption
4.1
holds. Suppose also that, for some

\alpha\in(0,1/2)
and any fixed
l\geq 1
,
uniformly over
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with

|S|\leq l
and over
i,j\notin S
with
i\neq j
, we have

(88)

R_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\prec n^{-\alpha},\qquad R_{ij}^{(S)}\prec n^{-\alpha}.

Set
\alpha^{\prime}=\min(\frac{3\alpha}{2},\frac{1}{2})
.
Then for any fixed
l\geq 1
, uniformly over
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with

|S|\leq l
and over
i,j\notin S
with
i\neq j
,

R_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}},\qquad R_{ij}^{(S)}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}.

Before proving this lemma, we derive an estimate (Lemma
4.12

below) that we will use in conjunction with the fluctuation averaging result of
Lemma
3.5
. Define

(89)

\mathbb{E}_{i}[\cdot]=\mathbb{E}[\;\cdot\mid H^{(i)}],\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{i}=1-\mathbb{E}_{i},

where
\mathbb{E}_{i}
is the expectation over the entries in only row and
column
i
of
H
. Note that

\mathbb{E}_{i}\mathbb{E}_{j}=\mathbb{E}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{i}
for all
i\neq j
, so
\{\mathbb{E}_{i},\mathcal{Q}_{i}:i=1,\ldots,n\}
form a
commuting system of projections. Set
\mathcal{Q}_{T}=\prod_{i\in T}\mathcal{Q}_{i}
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Under Assumption
4.1
, for any fixed
l\geq 1
, uniformly over

S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l
and
i\notin S
,

(90)

(R_{ii}^{(S)})^{-1}\prec 1.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}
be the
i^{\text{th}}
column of
H^{(iS)}
, having
entries
(\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)})_{j}=h_{ji}
if
j\notin S\cup\{i\}
and 0
otherwise. Lemma
3.7
(a) gives

\displaystyle\|(R_{ii}^{(S)})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq|h_{ii}|\left\|\mathsf{x}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\|\mathsf{z}_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|(\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\otimes\mathsf{x})^{*}R^{(iS)}(\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\otimes\mathsf{x})\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq|h_{ii}|\left\|\mathsf{x}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\|\mathsf{z}_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|R^{(iS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\mathsf{x}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\|_{2}^{2}.

We have
\|\mathsf{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\mathsf{z}_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec 1
by (
43
),

h_{ii}\prec n^{-1/2}
by (
16
), and

\|\mathbf{h}_{i}^{(iS)}\|_{2}\prec 1

by Lemma
3.4
(a) in the scalar case of
\mathcal{X}=\mathbb{C}
,
so the result follows.
∎

Lemma 4.12
.

For any
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
r,j\notin S
with
r\neq j
, define

\mathcal{Z}_{rj}^{(S)}=\mathcal{Q}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(S)}R_{rj}^{(S)*}].

Fix any
l\geq 1
. Under the assumptions of Lemma
4.10
,
uniformly over subsets
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with

|S|\leq l
,
r,j\notin S
with
r\neq j
, and

T\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}\setminus(S\cup\{r,j\})
with
|T|\leq l
,

\mathcal{Q}_{T}[\mathcal{Z}_{rj}^{(S)}]\prec n^{-(2+|T|)\alpha}.

Proof.

For
|T|=0
, we have
R_{rj}^{(S)}R_{rj}^{(S)*}\prec n^{-2\alpha}
by the
assumption (
88
) and Hölder’s inequality. Then also

\mathcal{Z}_{rj}^{(S)}=R_{rj}^{(S)}R_{rj}^{(S)*}-\mathbb{E}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(S)}R_{rj}^{(S)*}]\prec n^{-2\alpha}
by Lemma
3.2
(c), so the assertion holds
for
|T|=0
.

If
|T|\geq 1
, suppose
i_{1}\in T
. Then Lemma
3.7
(c)
gives

\displaystyle R_{rj}^{(S)}R_{rj}^{(S)*}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{R_{rj}^{(i_{1}S)}R_{rj}^{(i_{1}S)*}}_{=L(\{i_{1}\})}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\Big(R_{ri_{1}}^{(S)}\frac{1}{R_{i_{1}i_{1}}^{(S)}}R_{i_{1}j}^{(S)}\Big)R_{rj}^{(i_{1}S)*}+R_{rj}^{(i_{1}S)}\Big(R_{ri_{1}}^{(S)}\frac{1}{R_{i_{1}i_{1}}^{(S)}}R_{i_{1}j}^{(S)}\Big)^{*}+\Big(R_{ri_{1}}^{(S)}\frac{1}{R_{i_{1}i_{1}}^{(S)}}R_{i_{1}j}^{(S)}\Big)\Big(R_{ri_{1}}^{(S)}\frac{1}{R_{i_{1}i_{1}}^{(S)}}R_{i_{1}j}^{(S)}\Big)^{*}}_{=P(\{i_{1}\})}.

Here, the first term
L(\{i_{1}\})
is independent of the entries of row and
column
i_{1}
of
H
, so
\mathcal{Q}_{i_{1}}[L(\{i_{1}\})]=0
. The remaining
terms constituting
P(\{i_{1}\})
each have at least 3 off-diagonal resolvent
factors, i.e. factors of the form

R_{pq}^{(S^{\prime})}
or
R_{pq}^{(S^{\prime})*}
for some
p\neq q
and
p,q\notin S^{\prime}
,
so (
90
) and
(
88
) imply
P(\{i_{1}\})\prec n^{-3\alpha}
.

Now if
|T|\geq 2
and
i_{2}\in T
with
i_{2}\neq i_{1}
, we
apply Lemma
3.7
(c) again
to expand each factor of
P(\{i_{1}\})
over
i_{2}
, yielding

\displaystyle R_{rj}^{(S)}R_{rj}^{(S)*}=L(\{i_{1},i_{2}\})+P(\{i_{1},i_{2}\})

where

\displaystyle L(\{i_{1},i_{2}\})

\displaystyle=L(\{i_{1}\})+\Big(R_{ri_{1}}^{(i_{2}S)}\frac{1}{R_{i_{1}i_{1}}^{(i_{2}S)}}R_{i_{1}j}^{(i_{2}S)}\Big)R_{rj}^{(i_{1}i_{2}S)*}+R_{rj}^{(i_{1}i_{2}S)}\Big(R_{ri_{1}}^{(i_{2}S)}\frac{1}{R_{i_{1}i_{1}}^{(i_{2}S)}}R_{i_{1}j}^{(i_{2}S)}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\Big(R_{ri_{1}}^{(i_{2}S)}\frac{1}{R_{i_{1}i_{1}}^{(i_{2}S)}}R_{i_{1}j}^{(i_{2}S)}\Big)\Big(R_{ri_{1}}^{(i_{2}S)}\frac{1}{R_{i_{1}i_{1}}^{(i_{2}S)}}R_{i_{1}j}^{(i_{2}S)}\Big)^{*}

and
P(\{i_{1},i_{2}\})
collects all remaining terms in the expansion of

P(\{i_{1}\})
. Each term of
L(\{i_{1},i_{2}\})
is independent of the
entries of either row and column
i_{1}
or
i_{2}
of
H
, so

Q_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}[L(\{i_{1},i_{2}\})]=0
.
Each term of
P(\{i_{1},i_{2}\})
has at least 4 off-diagonal resolvent factors,
so
P(\{i_{1},i_{2}\})\prec n^{-4\alpha}
. Inductively applying
Lemma (
3.7
)(c) to expand
P(\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\})
in each
successive index
i_{k+1}
of
T
, this shows

\displaystyle R_{rj}^{(S)}R_{rj}^{(S)*}=L(T)+P(T)

where

•

Each term in
L(T)
is independent of the entries of
row and column
i
of
H

for at least one index
i\in T
, so
\mathcal{Q}_{T}[L(T)]=0
.

•

P(T)
is a sum of at most
C_{l}
summands, each summand a product
of at most
C_{l}
factors, for a constant
C_{l}>0
depending only on the given
upper bound
l
for
|T|
.

•

Each term of
P(T)
has at least
2+|T|
off-diagonal resolvent
factors, and hence by (
88
)
is of size
O_{\prec}(n^{-(2+|T|)\alpha})
.

Then
P(T)\prec n^{-(2+|T|)\alpha}
, uniformly over
T\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}\setminus(S\cup\{r,j\})
with
|T|\leq l
.
This implies
\mathcal{Q}_{T\cup\{r\}}[L(T)]=0
and

\mathcal{Q}_{T\cup\{r\}}[P(T)]\prec n^{-(2+|T|)\alpha}
by
Lemma
3.2
(c), so

\mathcal{Q}_{T}[\mathcal{Z}_{rj}^{(T)}]=\mathcal{Q}_{T\cup\{r\}}[R_{rj}^{(T)}R_{rj}^{(T)*}]\prec n^{-(2+|T|)\alpha}
as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.10
.

In view of Lemma
3.7
(b) for the form of

R_{ij}^{(S)}
, we consider first the quantity

\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}

for
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l
and
i,j\notin S

with
i\neq j
.
Recalling the assumption
\mathbb{E}[(\sqrt{n}|h_{ij}|)^{p}]\leq C_{p}
and
applying Lemma
3.4
(a) conditional on
H^{(iS)}
,
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
and a constant
C_{p}>0
,

(91)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]\leq C_{p}n^{-p/2}\max\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg],\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]\bigg\}.

The second bound of (
91
)
may be controlled spectrally: Using that

R^{(iS)}_{rj}=0
for
r\in S\cup\{i\}
and
j\notin S\cup\{i\}
,

\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\Big\|(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes 1)R^{(iS)*}R^{(iS)}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes 1)\Big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|R^{(iS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\delta^{-2}.

Thus, applying monotonicity of the
L^{p}
-norm in
p

(Lemma
D.2
),

(92)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{p/2}\bigg]\leq\delta^{-p}.

For the first bound of (
91
), let us write

\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}=R_{jj}^{(iS)}R_{jj}^{(iS)*}+\sum_{r}^{(ijS)}\mathbb{E}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}]+\sum_{r}^{(ijS)}\mathcal{Q}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}].

Applying
\|R_{jj}^{(iS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
for the first term, and
Lemma
3.5
(a) with the estimates of Lemma

4.12
for the third term, this gives

(93)

\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}=\sum_{r}^{(ijS)}\mathbb{E}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}]+O_{\prec}(1+n^{1-3\alpha}).

We expand the resolvent for the remaining term of (
93
),
applying Lemma
3.4
(b) to write, for any
r\notin S\cup\{i,j\}
,

R_{rj}^{(iS)}={-}R_{rr}^{(iS)}\mathsf{x}\sum_{s}^{(irS)}h_{rs}R_{sj}^{(irS)}.

Here
R_{rj}^{(iS)}\prec n^{-\alpha}
by the second statement of
(
88
),
so multiplying by
(R_{rr}^{(iS)})^{-1}
and
\mathsf{x}^{-1}
and applying
(
90
) and
\|\mathsf{x}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\gamma
, also

\sum_{s}^{(irS)}h_{rs}R_{sj}^{(irS)}\prec n^{-\alpha}
.
Then the first statement of (
88
) gives

R_{rj}^{(iS)}={-}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}\sum_{s}^{(irS)}h_{rs}R_{sj}^{(irS)}+O_{\prec}(n^{-2\alpha}).

Applying this and independence of
R_{sj}^{(irS)}
with the variables in
row/column
r
of
H
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}]

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}\bigg(\sum_{s,t}^{(irS)}\mathbb{E}[h_{rs}h_{tr}]R_{sj}^{(irS)}R_{jt}^{(irS)*}\bigg)\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}^{*}+O_{\prec}(n^{-3\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}\bigg(\sum_{s}^{(irS)}R_{sj}^{(irS)}R_{js}^{(irS)*}\bigg)\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}^{*}+O_{\prec}(n^{-3\alpha}).

Recalling
r\notin S\cup\{i,j\}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s}^{(irS)}R_{sj}^{(irS)}R_{js}^{(irS)*}

\displaystyle=R_{jj}^{(irS)}R_{jj}^{(irS)*}+\sum_{s}^{(ijrS)}R_{sj}^{(irS)}R_{js}^{(irS)*}

\displaystyle=R_{jj}^{(irS)}R_{jj}^{(irS)*}+\sum_{s}^{(ijrS)}R_{sj}^{(iS)}R_{js}^{(iS)*}+O_{\prec}(n^{1-3\alpha})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s}^{(iS)}R_{sj}^{(iS)}R_{js}^{(iS)*}+O_{\prec}(1+n^{1-3\alpha}),

the second line using

R_{sj}^{(irS)}=R_{sj}^{(iS)}+O_{\prec}(n^{-2\alpha})

for
r\notin S\cup\{i,j,s\}
by Lemma

3.7
(c), (
88
), and
(
90
), and the third line using
\|R_{jj}^{(irS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
and
\|R_{sj}^{(iS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
for
s\in\{j,r\}
.
So

\mathbb{E}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}]=\frac{1}{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}\bigg(\sum_{s}^{(iS)}R_{sj}^{(iS)}R_{js}^{(iS)*}\bigg)\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}^{*}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1}+n^{-3\alpha}).

Summing over
r\notin S\cup\{i,j\}
, this gives

\displaystyle\sum_{r}^{(ijS)}\mathbb{E}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{r}^{(ijS)}\frac{1}{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}\bigg(\sum_{s}^{(iS)}R_{sj}^{(iS)}R_{js}^{(iS)*}\bigg)\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}^{*}+O_{\prec}(1+n^{1-3\alpha})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}\bigg(\sum_{s}^{(iS)}R_{sj}^{(iS)}R_{js}^{(iS)*}\bigg)\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}^{*}+O_{\prec}(1+n^{1-3\alpha}),

where the second step applies
the trivial bound
\|\sum_{s}^{(iS)}R_{sj}^{(iS)}R_{js}^{(iS)*}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n\|R^{(iS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq n\delta^{-2}
to include the summands for

r\in S\cup\{i,j\}
with an additional
O_{\prec}(1)
error. Applying this back to (
93
), we
obtain

\mathcal{L}_{2}\bigg(\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}\bigg)\prec 1+n^{1-3\alpha}

where here
\mathcal{L}_{2}
is the linear operator of Lemma
4.7
.
By the quantitative invertibility of
\mathcal{L}_{2}
established in
Lemma
4.7
, this implies

\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}\prec 1+n^{1-3\alpha}.

Then, for any fixed
p\in[2,\infty)
, we get (from this and Lemma

3.2
)

(94)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)}R_{rj}^{(iS)*}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]\prec 1+n^{(1-3\alpha)(p/2)},

which controls the first bound of (
91
).

Then, applying (
94
) and
(
92
) to
(
91
), for any fixed
p\in[2,\infty)
and
all
n\geq n_{0}(p)
,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]\leq n^{-p\alpha^{\prime}+0.1}

where we have set
\alpha^{\prime}=\min(\frac{3\alpha}{2},\frac{1}{2})
.
Then, for any fixed
q\in[1,\infty)
and
\epsilon,D>0
, choosing
p\geq q

large enough so that
p\epsilon>D+0.1
and applying Markov’s inequality and
monotonicity of
\|\cdot\|_{p}
in
p
(Lemma
D.2
),
for all
n\geq n_{0}(q,\epsilon,D)
,

(95)

\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\bigg\|_{q}\geq n^{-\alpha^{\prime}+\epsilon}\bigg]\leq n^{p\alpha^{\prime}-p\epsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\bigg\|_{q}^{p}\bigg]\leq n^{p\alpha^{\prime}-p\epsilon}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]<n^{-D}.

This shows
\sum_{r}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(iS)}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}
.
Then by Lemma
3.7
(b) and the bound

\|R_{ii}^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}
, we have the improved estimate
(uniformly over
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l
and

i,j\notin S
with
i\neq j
)

(96)

R_{ij}^{(S)}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}.

Now to show
R_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}
, recall from
(
80
) that

\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}=\bigg({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\Delta_{i}^{(S)}-\mathsf{x}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{R}_{jj}^{(S)}\bigg]\mathsf{x}\bigg)^{-1}

where
\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}=R_{ii}^{(S)}
for all
i\notin S
, and

\Delta_{i}^{(S)}
is the error defined by (
81
)
and (
82
). For
the term
\mathrm{I}
of (
81
), applying
Lemma
3.4
(a) conditional on
H^{(iS)}
gives,
for any
p\geq 2
and a constant
C_{p}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}\left(|h_{ir}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)R_{rr}^{(iS)}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}n^{-p}\max\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rr}^{(iS)}R_{rr}^{(iS)*}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg],\;\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r}^{(iS)}R_{rr}^{(iS)*}R_{rr}^{(iS)}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}n^{-p}\max\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r,s=1}^{n}R_{rs}^{(iS)}R_{rs}^{(iS)*}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg],\;\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r,s=1}^{n}R_{rs}^{(iS)*}R_{rs}^{(iS)}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]\bigg\}

where the second inequality applies monotonicity of the operator square-root
0\leq\mathsf{x}\leq\mathsf{y}\Rightarrow\mathsf{x}^{1/2}\leq\mathsf{y}^{1/2}
and monotonicity of the

L^{p}
-norm over the positive cone (Lemma
D.2
). Similarly
applying Lemma
3.4
(c) conditional on
H^{(iS)}
for the
summation over
r\neq s
, the term
\mathrm{I}
of (
81
) is bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}\left(|h_{ir}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)R_{rr}^{(iS)}+\sum_{r\neq s}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rs}^{(iS)}h_{si}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}n^{-p}\max\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r,s=1}^{n}R_{rs}^{(iS)}R_{rs}^{(iS)*}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg],\;\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r,s=1}^{n}R_{rs}^{(iS)*}R_{rs}^{(iS)}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg],

(97)

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54ptn\,\mathbb{E}[\|R^{(iS)}-\operatorname{diag}(R^{(iS)})\|_{p}^{p}],\;n\,\mathbb{E}[\|{R^{(iS)}}^{\mathsf{t}}-\operatorname{diag}(R^{(iS)})\|_{p}^{p}]\bigg\}

where
{R^{(iS)}}^{\mathsf{t}}=\sum_{k,l=1}^{n}E_{kl}\otimes R^{(iS)}_{lk}

is the partial transpose of
R^{(iS)}
as defined in
(
38
), and here

\operatorname{diag}(R^{(iS)})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}E_{kk}\otimes R_{kk}^{(iS)}
is the operator that has only the diagonal entries
of
R^{(iS)}
. The first three bounds on the right side of
(
97
) may be controlled spectrally: For the third bound,

(98)

n\,\mathbb{E}[\|R^{(iS)}-\operatorname{diag}(R^{(iS)})\|_{p}^{p}]\leq n\,\mathbb{E}[(\|R^{(iS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\operatorname{diag}(R^{(iS)})\|_{\mathrm{op}})^{p}]\leq n(2\delta^{-1})^{p}.

For the first and second bounds,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{r,s=1}^{n}R_{rs}^{(iS)}R_{rs}^{(iS)*}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=n\,\Big\|(n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\otimes 1)R^{(iS)}R^{(iS)*}\Big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n\|R^{(iS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq n\delta^{-2},

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{r,s=1}^{n}R_{rs}^{(iS)*}R_{rs}^{(iS)}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=n\,\Big\|(n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\otimes 1)R^{(iS)*}R^{(iS)}\Big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq n\|R^{(iS)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq n\delta^{-2}.

Thus

(99)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r,s=1}^{n}R_{rs}^{(iS)}R_{rs}^{(iS)*}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg],\;\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{r,s=1}^{n}R_{rs}^{(iS)*}R_{rs}^{(iS)}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]\leq n^{p/2}\delta^{-p}.

For the fourth bound
n\,\mathbb{E}[\|{R^{(iS)}}^{\mathsf{t}}-\operatorname{diag}(R^{(iS)})\|_{p}^{p}]
of (
97
),
we apply the following argument:
For any
M=\sum_{j,k}E_{jk}\otimes M_{jk}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}\otimes\mathcal{X}
,
note that

M^{\mathsf{t}}-\operatorname{diag}(M)=\sum_{1\leq j\neq k\leq n}E_{jk}\otimes M_{kj}=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}M[j],\qquad\text{ for }M[j]=\sum_{k=1}^{n}E_{j+k,k}\otimes M_{k,j+k}

where
j+k
is interpreted modulo
n
. Here

\displaystyle\|M[j]\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=\|(M[j]M[j]^{*})^{1/2}\|_{p}^{p}=\bigg\|\sum_{k=1}^{n}E_{j+k,j+k}\otimes(M_{k,j+k}M_{k,j+k}^{*})^{1/2}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=(n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\otimes\tau)\sum_{k=1}^{n}E_{j+k,j+k}\otimes(M_{k,j+k}M_{k,j+k}^{*})^{p/2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|M_{k,j+k}\|_{p}^{p}\leq\max_{k=1}^{n}\|M_{k,j+k}\|_{p}^{p},

so

\|M^{\mathsf{t}}-\operatorname{diag}(M)\|_{p}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\|M[j]\|_{p}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{n}\|M_{k,j+k}\|_{p}\Big).

Applying this to
M=R^{(iS)}
, observe that for each
j=1,\ldots,n-1
we have

R^{(iS)}_{k,j+k}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}
if
k,j+k\notin S\cup\{i\}
as
already shown in (
96
),
and
R^{(iS)}_{k,j+k}=0
by definition if
k\in S\cup\{i\}
or
j+k\in S\cup\{i\}
with
k\neq j+k
. Thus

\max_{k}\|R_{k,j+k}^{(iS)}\|_{p}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}
, so

\|{R^{(iS)}}^{\mathsf{t}}\|_{p}\prec n^{1-\alpha^{\prime}}
, and

(100)

n\,\mathbb{E}[\|{R^{(iS)}}^{\mathsf{t}}\|_{p}^{p}]\prec n\cdot n^{p(1-\alpha^{\prime})}.

Applying (
98
), (
99
),
and (
100
) to
(
97
), for any fixed
p\in[2,\infty)

and all
n\geq n_{0}(p)
,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}\left(|h_{ir}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)R_{rr}^{(iS)}+\sum_{r\neq s}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rs}^{(iS)}h_{si}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\bigg]\leq n^{\max(1-p,-p/2,1-p\alpha^{\prime})+0.1}=n^{-p\alpha^{\prime}+1.1},

the last equality using
\alpha^{\prime}\leq 1/2
.
Then, for any fixed
q\in[1,\infty)
and

\epsilon,D>0
, applying this with
p\geq q
large enough so that

p\epsilon>D+1.1
, we obtain similarly to (
95
)

\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{r}^{(iS)}\left(|h_{ir}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)R_{rr}^{(iS)}+\sum_{r\neq s}^{(iS)}h_{ir}R_{rs}^{(iS)}h_{si}\bigg\|_{q}\geq n^{-\alpha^{\prime}+\epsilon}\bigg]<n^{-D}.

Hence, for the term
\mathrm{I}
defining
\Delta_{i}^{(S)}
in
(
81
), we have
\mathrm{I}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}
.

For the other terms of (
81
), we have

\mathrm{II},\mathrm{IV}\prec n^{-1}
by (
83
),
and
\mathrm{III}\prec n^{-2\alpha^{\prime}}
by its form (
84
)
and (
96
) and (
90
).
Applying also
h_{ii}\prec n^{-1/2}
, this gives (uniformly over

i=1,\ldots,n
)

(101)

\Delta_{i}^{(S)}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}.

Then, setting
\Delta^{(S)}=\sum_{i}E_{ii}\otimes\Delta_{i}^{(S)}
, we have

\|\Delta^{(S)}\|_{p}^{p}=n^{-1}\sum_{i}\|\Delta_{i}^{(S)}\|_{p}^{p}
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
so
also
\|\Delta^{(S)}\|_{p}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}
for each fixed
p
.
Recalling the weak estimate for

\|\Delta^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
from (
85
) on the high probability event

\mathcal{E}
, which ensures
\Im(\mathsf{z}+\Delta^{(S)})\geq\delta/2
,
this implies by (
86
) and
Corollary
4.4
(a) applied now in the
norm
\|\cdot\|_{p}
that

\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ii}\otimes\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-\mathsf{r}_{0}\bigg\|_{p}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}.

Thus, for any
i\notin S
, any
q\in[1,\infty)
, and any
\epsilon,D>0
,
choosing
p\geq q
large enough such that
p\epsilon>1.1
,

\|R_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\|_{q}^{p}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\|_{p}^{p}=n\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ii}\otimes\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-\mathsf{r}_{0}\bigg\|_{p}^{p}\leq n^{-\alpha^{\prime}p+1.1}<n^{(-\alpha^{\prime}+\epsilon)p}

with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
for
n\geq n_{0}(q,\epsilon,D)
.
This shows
R_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\prec n^{-\alpha^{\prime}}
.
∎

4.4.
Proof of Theorem
4.2

We now prove the main result of this section,
Theorem
4.2
.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
, (
45
–
46
).

Lemma
4.9
implies

R_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\prec n^{-\alpha}
and
R_{ij}^{(S)}\prec n^{-\alpha}
for some
\alpha>0
, uniformly over sets
S
with
|S|\leq l
and
i,j\notin S
with
i\neq j
. Then, iterating
Lemma
4.10
a constant number of times, we get

R_{ii}^{(S)}-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\prec n^{-1/2}
and
R_{ij}^{(S)}\prec n^{-1/2}
,
implying for
S=\emptyset
the statements
(
45
–
46
).
∎

For the remaining statements of Theorem
4.2
, we collect here
several estimates needed for additional applications of fluctuation averaging
(Lemma
3.5
).

Lemma 4.13
.

Under Assumption
4.1
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{i}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathcal{Q}_{i}[R_{ii}^{-1}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Q}_{i}\mathcal{Q}_{j}[R_{ij}]\text{ for }i\neq j,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{r}^{(ij)}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathcal{Q}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(i)}]\text{ for distinct }r,i,j.

Fix any
l\geq 1
.

(a)

Uniformly over
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l

and
i\notin S
,
\mathcal{Q}_{S}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}]\prec n^{-1/2-|S|/2}
.

(b)

Uniformly over
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l

and
i,j\notin S
with
i\neq j
,
\mathcal{Q}_{S}[\mathcal{Z}_{ij}]\prec n^{-1/2-|S|/2}
.

(c)

Uniformly over
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l

and distinct
i,j,r\notin S
,
\mathcal{Q}_{S}[\mathcal{K}_{r}^{(ij)}]\prec n^{-1/2-|S|/2}
.

Proof.

For (a), since
\mathsf{r}_{0}
does not depend on
H
, we have

\mathcal{Q}_{S}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}]=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathcal{Q}_{S\cup\{i\}}[R_{ii}^{-1}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}
,
so by Hölder’s inequality and the bound

\|(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta
it suffices to show

\mathcal{Q}_{S}[\mathcal{Q}_{i}[R_{ii}^{-1}]]\prec n^{-1/2-|S|/2}.

We remark that for
S=\emptyset
, this follows from

\mathcal{Q}_{i}[R_{ii}^{-1}]=-\Delta_{i}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1})\prec n^{-1/2}
(c.f. (
106
) and (
103
) below).
For (c), similarly it suffices to show

\mathcal{Q}_{S}[\mathcal{Q}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(i)}]]\prec n^{-1/2-|S|/2}.

The argument for all three parts is then the same as in Lemma

4.12
, applying Lemma
3.7
(c) to
iteratively expand
R_{ii}^{-1}
,
R_{ij}
, and
R_{rj}^{(i)}
in the indices
of
S
, and applying now the optimal estimates

R_{pq}^{(S^{\prime})}\prec n^{-1/2}
from (
46
)
to bound the terms of the expansion. We omit further details for brevity.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.2
, (
44
).

Let us specialize (
81
) to
S=\emptyset
, and write
simply
\Delta_{i}=\Delta_{i}^{(S)}
. Then

(102)

\Delta_{i}={-}h_{ii}\mathsf{x}+\mathsf{x}\Bigg[\sum_{r}^{(i)}\left(|h_{ir}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)R_{rr}^{(i)}+\sum_{r\neq s}^{(i)}h_{ir}R_{rs}^{(i)}h_{si}\Bigg]\mathsf{x}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1}),

using the bounds
\mathrm{II}\prec n^{-1}
from (
83
) and
and
\mathrm{III}\prec n^{-1}
from (
84
),
(
46
), and (
90
). Furthermore,
(
101
) from the final iteration of bootstrapping with

\alpha^{\prime}=1/2
shows

(103)

\Delta_{i}\prec n^{-1/2}.

Define

(104)

\widehat{R}_{ii}=\bigg({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}R_{jj}\bigg]\mathsf{x}\bigg)^{-1}.

By (
45
) already shown, we have

R_{ii}=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})
uniformly in
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Then averaging over
i
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R_{ii}=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}\otimes 1[\mathsf{r}_{0}]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})=\mathsf{m}_{0}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

Applying this, the identity
\mathsf{a}^{-1}-\mathsf{b}^{-1}=\mathsf{a}^{-1}(\mathsf{b}-\mathsf{a})\mathsf{b}^{-1}
, and

\|\widehat{R}_{ii}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|(-\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\mathsf{m}_{0}\mathsf{x})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-1}

by Lemma
3.6
, we may approximate
\widehat{R}_{ii}

in (
104
) as

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{ii}=(-\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\mathsf{m}_{0}\mathsf{x}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}))^{-1}=(-\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\mathsf{m}_{0}\mathsf{x})^{-1}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}),

the last identity using the characterization of
\mathsf{r}_{0}
via the fixed-point
equation of Lemma
4.3
(a). Therefore,
applying again
\mathsf{a}^{-1}-\mathsf{b}^{-1}=\mathsf{a}^{-1}(\mathsf{b}-\mathsf{a})\mathsf{b}^{-1}

with
\widehat{R}_{ii}^{-1}=R_{ii}^{-1}+\Delta_{i}
from
(
80
), we have

(105)

R_{ii}=\widehat{R}_{ii}+R_{ii}\Delta_{i}\widehat{R}_{ii}=\widehat{R}_{ii}+(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\Delta_{i}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1})

where this applies (
45
) and
(
103
) to bound the error.

Comparing the form
(
102
) for
\Delta_{i}
with the expansion of
R_{ii}^{-1}
in
Lemma
3.7
(a), and noting that
\mathcal{Q}_{i}[\mathsf{z}_{i}]=0
because

\mathsf{z}_{i}
does not depend on
H
, we have

(106)

\Delta_{i}={-}\mathcal{Q}_{i}[R_{ii}^{-1}]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1}).

Thus, setting
\mathcal{Z}_{i}=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathcal{Q}_{i}[R_{ii}^{-1}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}
and averaging
again over
i
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R_{ii}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{R}_{ii}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{Z}_{i}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1}).

By Lemma
3.5
(a) (or (b)) applied with the estimates of
Lemma
4.13
(a), we have
n^{-1}\sum_{i}\mathcal{Z}_{i}\prec n^{-1}
.
Thus, defining

\widehat{\Delta}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R_{ii}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{R}_{ii}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R_{ii}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg({-}\mathsf{z}_{i}-\mathsf{x}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}R_{jj}\bigg]\mathsf{x}\bigg)^{-1},

this shows
\widehat{\Delta}\prec n^{-1}
. Observe also that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\bigg\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(R_{ii}-\widehat{R}_{ii})\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\bigg\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R_{ii}\Delta_{i}\widehat{R}_{ii}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\delta^{-2}\max_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\Delta_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

By the weak bound (
85
) for
\|\Delta_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
on
a high-probability event
\mathcal{E}
, this implies

\Im(\mathsf{z}_{i}+\mathsf{x}\widehat{\Delta}\mathsf{x})\geq\delta/2
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
and all
n\geq n_{0}(\gamma,\delta)
.
On this event
\mathcal{E}
,
Corollary
4.4
(b) implies,
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
,

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R_{ii}-\mathsf{m}_{0}\right\|_{p}\leq(1+2\gamma^{2}\delta^{-2})\|\widehat{\Delta}\|_{p}.

Thus
(n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\otimes 1)[R]-\mathsf{m}_{0}\prec n^{-1}
, showing
(
44
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.2
,
(
47
).

By the polarization identity

\displaystyle(\mathbf{u}\otimes 1)^{*}R(\mathbf{v}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\Big[((\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{u})\otimes 1)^{*}R((\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{u})\otimes 1)-((\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u})\otimes 1)^{*}R((\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u})\otimes 1)

\displaystyle\qquad+i((\mathbf{v}+i\mathbf{u})\otimes 1)^{*}R((\mathbf{v}+i\mathbf{u})\otimes 1)-i((\mathbf{v}-i\mathbf{u})\otimes 1)^{*}R((\mathbf{v}-i\mathbf{u})\otimes 1)\Big]

it suffices to prove the result uniformly over
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
satisfying

\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{2}\upsilon
. We have

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}\otimes 1)^{*}R(\mathbf{v}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}|v_{i}|^{2}R_{ii}+\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}R_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}|v_{i}|^{2}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}+\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}R_{ij}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{v}\otimes 1)^{*}\mathsf{r}_{0}(\mathbf{v}\otimes 1)+\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}R_{ij}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})

where the second line applies
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\prec 1
and
(
45
). It remains to
show
\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}R_{ij}\prec n^{-1/2}
.

Separating
R_{ij}
into its conditional mean and fluctuations,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}R_{ij}=\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}\mathbb{E}_{i}\mathbb{E}_{j}[R_{ij}]+\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}\mathbb{E}_{i}\mathcal{Q}_{j}[R_{ij}]+\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}\mathbb{E}_{j}\mathcal{Q}_{i}[R_{ij}]+\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}\mathcal{Q}_{i}\mathcal{Q}_{j}[R_{ij}].

We first examine
\mathbb{E}_{i}[R_{ij}]
. Recall
\widehat{R}_{ii}
from
(
104
). Observe that

R_{ii}=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})
by
(
45
),
whereas
\widehat{R}_{ii}=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1})
with the smaller error

n^{-1}
by its definition and the estimate
(
44
) already shown. Then,
applying Lemma
3.7
(b)
and (
105
) to expand
R_{ij}
,

\displaystyle{-}\mathbb{E}_{i}[R_{ij}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{i}\left[R_{ii}\mathsf{x}\sum_{r}^{(i)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(i)}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{i}\left[\widehat{R}_{ii}\mathsf{x}\sum_{r}^{(i)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(i)}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{i}\left[R_{ii}\Delta_{i}\widehat{R}_{ii}\mathsf{x}\sum_{r}^{(i)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(i)}\right]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{i}\left[(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\sum_{r}^{(i)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(i)}\right]}_{=\mathrm{I}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{i}\left[(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\Delta_{i}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\sum_{r}^{(i)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(i)}\right]}_{=\mathrm{II}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{i}\left[O_{\prec}(n^{-1})\cdot\mathsf{x}\sum_{r}^{(i)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(i)}\right]}_{=\mathrm{III}}.

In these expressions, by (
46
),
(
90
), and Lemma
3.7
(b), we have

(107)

\sum_{r}^{(i)}h_{ir}R_{rj}^{(i)}={-}\mathsf{x}^{-1}R_{ii}^{-1}R_{ij}\prec n^{-1/2}

so
\mathrm{III}\prec n^{-3/2}
.
Since
R^{(i)}_{rj}
is a function only of
H^{(i)}

(and
\mathsf{r}_{0},\mathsf{x}
do not depend on
H
), it follows from
\mathbb{E}[h_{ir}]=0

that
\mathrm{I}=0
. For
\mathrm{II}
, recall the form of

\Delta_{i}
from (
102
).
Substituting this expression of
\Delta_{i}
into
\mathrm{II}
gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle=-(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\sum_{r}^{(i)}\mathbb{E}_{i}[h_{ii}h_{ir}]R_{rj}^{(i)}+\sum_{t,r}^{(i)}\mathbb{E}_{i}\left[\left(|h_{it}|^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\right)h_{ir}\right](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}R_{tt}^{(i)}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}R_{rj}^{(i)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{r}^{(i)}\sum_{t\neq s}^{(i)}\mathbb{E}_{i}[h_{it}h_{si}h_{ir}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}R_{ts}^{(i)}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}R_{rj}^{(i)}+O_{\prec}(n^{-3/2}).

The first term is 0 since
r\neq i
.
Similarly, the second term is 0 for summands
r\neq t
,
and the third term is 0 since at least one of
r,s,t
is distinct from
the other two. Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}=\sum_{r}^{(i)}\mathbb{E}_{i}[|h_{ir}|^{2}h_{ir}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}R_{rr}^{(i)}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}R_{rj}^{(i)}+O_{\prec}(n^{-3/2}).

Note that the single summand for
r=j
is
O_{\prec}(n^{-3/2})
,
that all summands for
r\neq j
are
O_{\prec}(n^{-2})
by (
46
), and that

R_{rr}^{(i)}=R_{rr}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1})=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})
. Then
we may further write this as

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r}^{(ij)}\mathbb{E}_{i}[|h_{ir}|^{2}h_{ir}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}R_{rr}^{(i)}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}R_{rj}^{(i)}+O_{\prec}(n^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r}^{(ij)}\mathbb{E}_{i}[|h_{ir}|^{2}h_{ir}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}R_{rj}^{(i)}+O_{\prec}(n^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r}^{(ij)}\mathbb{E}_{i}[|h_{ir}|^{2}h_{ir}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}(\mathbb{E}_{r}+\mathcal{Q}_{r})[R_{rj}^{(i)}]+O_{\prec}(n^{-3/2}).

By Lemma
3.5
(a) applied with the estimates of Lemma

4.13
(c), we have

\sum_{r}^{(ij)}\mathbb{E}_{i}[|h_{ir}|^{2}h_{ir}](\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{ii}\mathsf{x}\mathcal{Q}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(i)}]\prec n^{-3/2}.

We now examine
\mathbb{E}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(i)}]
.
Again by Lemmas
3.7
(b) and
3.2
(c),

\displaystyle{-}\mathbb{E}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(i)}]=\mathbb{E}_{r}\left[R_{rr}^{(i)}\mathsf{x}\sum_{s}^{(ir)}h_{rs}R_{sj}^{(ir)}\right]=(\mathsf{r}_{0})_{rr}\mathsf{x}\mathbb{E}_{r}\left[\sum_{s}^{(ir)}h_{rs}R_{sj}^{(ir)}\right]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1}).

This first term is 0, so
\mathbb{E}_{r}[R_{rj}^{(i)}]\prec n^{-1}
. Combining the
above gives
\mathrm{II}\prec n^{-3/2}
, and hence

\mathbb{E}_{i}[R_{ij}]\prec n^{-3/2}
. By symmetry, also
\mathbb{E}_{j}[R_{ij}]\prec n^{-3/2}
, so we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}R_{ij}=\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}\mathcal{Q}_{i}\mathcal{Q}_{j}[R_{ij}]+\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}\cdot O_{\prec}(n^{-3/2})=\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}\mathcal{Q}_{i}\mathcal{Q}_{j}[R_{ij}]+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}),

where the second equality applies
\sum_{i}|v_{i}|\leq\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\prec\sqrt{n}
.
Finally, by Lemma
3.5
(c) applied with the
estimates of Lemma
4.13
(b), we have

\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}\mathcal{Q}_{i}\mathcal{Q}_{j}[R_{ij}]\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\sum_{i\neq j}|v_{i}|^{2}|v_{j}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\prec n^{-1/2}.

So
\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{v}_{i}v_{j}R_{ij}\prec n^{-1/2}

as desired, completing the proof.
∎

5.
Analysis of the Kronecker deformed Wigner model

We now prove Proposition
2.3
and
Theorem
2.4
. For spectral arguments
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
,
recall the following quantities from Section
2
:

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle=A\otimes I+I\otimes B+\Theta\otimes\Xi\in\mathbb{C}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{q}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{a}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathsf{b}+\Theta\otimes\Xi\in\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A},

\displaystyle G(z)

\displaystyle=(Q-z\,I\otimes I)^{-1},\qquad m(z)=n^{-2}\operatorname{Tr}G(z),

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}(z)

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{q}-z\,1\otimes 1)^{-1},\qquad G_{0}(z)=(\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes\tau^{\mathcal{D}})[\mathsf{g}(z)],\qquad m_{0}(z)=\tau\otimes\tau[\mathsf{g}(z)].

We first show Proposition
2.3
and
all statements of Theorem
2.4
except the estimate

G_{ij,\alpha\beta}\prec n^{-1}
of (
27
) using
the analyses of Section
4
.

Proof of Proposition
2.3
.

We apply Lemma
4.3
(b) with
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{A}
,
\mathsf{x}=1_{\mathcal{A}}
, and

\mathsf{z}={-}I\otimes\mathsf{b}-\Theta\otimes\Xi+z\,I\otimes 1_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}\otimes\mathcal{A}.

Then
\Im\mathsf{z}=(\Im z)(I\otimes 1)
, so
\mathsf{z}_{i}={-}\mathsf{b}-\theta_{i}\Xi+z\in\mathcal{A}^{+}

for each
i=1,\ldots,n
. Then
Lemma
4.3
(b) ensures that

\mathsf{m}_{b}(z)=(\tau\otimes 1)[\mathsf{g}(z)]
is the unique fixed point
in
\mathcal{A}^{+}
to the fixed-point equation (
20
). Similarly

\mathsf{m}_{a}(z)=(1\otimes\tau)[\mathsf{g}(z)]
is the unique fixed point
in
\mathcal{A}^{+}
to (
19
), and the identity

m_{0}(z)=\tau[\mathsf{m}_{a}(z)]=\tau[\mathsf{m}_{b}(z)]
follows from taking a second trace
\tau

for either
\mathsf{m}_{a}
or
\mathsf{m}_{b}
.
Furthermore, Lemma
4.3
(a) shows that

(\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes 1)[\mathsf{g}(z)]=({-}\mathsf{z}-I\otimes\mathsf{m}_{b}(z))^{-1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ii}\otimes(\mathsf{b}+\theta_{i}\Xi-z-\mathsf{m}_{b}(z))^{-1}.

Then applying
1\otimes\tau^{\mathcal{D}}
shows

G_{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ii}\otimes\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\big[(\mathsf{b}+\theta_{i}\Xi-z-\mathsf{m}_{b}(z))^{-1}\big],

and similarly

G_{0}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\big[(\mathsf{a}+\xi_{\alpha}\Theta-z-\mathsf{m}_{a}(z))^{-1}\big]\otimes E_{\alpha\alpha}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
2.4
, (
21
–
26
) and (
28
).

Define

(108)

\tilde{\mathsf{g}}(z)=(\mathsf{a}\otimes I+1_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes B+\Theta\otimes\Xi-z1_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes I)^{-1}\in\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}

and abbreviate
G=G(z)
,
\tilde{\mathsf{g}}=\tilde{\mathsf{g}}(z)
, and
\mathsf{g}=\mathsf{g}(z)
.
Fix any
\epsilon>0
and consider the event
\mathcal{E}=\{\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\}
.
We apply Theorem
4.2
conditional on
B
and
this event
\mathcal{E}
, with
\mathcal{X}=\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
,
R=G
,
\mathsf{r}_{0}=\tilde{\mathsf{g}}
,

H=A
,
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{a}
,
\mathsf{x}=I
, and

\mathsf{z}={-}I\otimes B-\Theta\otimes\Xi+z\,I\otimes I.

Then Assumption
4.1
holds (for
\gamma=1
and modified
constants
\upsilon,\delta>0
).
Conditional on
B
and the event
\mathcal{E}
, Theorem
4.2
shows
over the randomness of
A
, uniformly
in
i\neq j
and
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
with
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2},\|\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq\upsilon
,

(109)

\begin{gathered}(n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\otimes I)[G]-(\tau\otimes I)[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}]\prec n^{-1},\\
G_{ii}-(\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes I)[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}]_{ii}\prec n^{-1/2},\qquad G_{ij}\prec n^{-1/2},\\
(\mathbf{u}\otimes I)^{*}G(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\otimes I)-(\mathbf{u}\otimes I)^{*}(\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes I)[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}](\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\otimes I)\prec n^{-1/2}.\end{gathered}

In light of the bound
G_{ii}^{-1}\prec 1
from (
90
),
the form
(\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes I)[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}]_{ii}=(B+\theta_{i}\Xi-z-M_{B}(z))^{-1}

from Lemma
4.3
(a)
where
M_{B}(z)=(\tau\otimes 1)[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}(z)]
,
and the identity
A^{-1}-B^{-1}=A^{-1}(B-A)B^{-1}
,
the second statement here shows also

G_{ii}^{-1}-\big((\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes I)[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}]_{ii}\big)^{-1}=G_{ii}^{-1}-(B+\theta_{i}\Xi-z-M_{B}(z))\prec n^{-1/2},

hence

G_{ii}^{-1}-G_{jj}^{-1}=(\theta_{i}-\theta_{j})\Xi+O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}).

As
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}]>1-n^{-D}
for any fixed
D>0
and all
n\geq n_{0}(D)
,
these statements then also hold unconditionally.
In particular, by Remark
3.3
this implies

\|G_{ij}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec n^{-1/2}
and

\|G_{ii}^{-1}-G_{jj}^{-1}-(\theta_{i}-\theta_{j})\Xi\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec n^{-1/2}
.
The argument for
G_{\alpha\alpha}
and
G_{\alpha\beta}
is symmetric,
so this shows (
22
–
24
).
The bounds (
26
) are an immediate consequence of
(
24
).

To prove the remaining statements (
21
),
(
25
), and (
28
), we
apply Theorem
4.2
again to the second tensor factor,
with
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{A}
,
R=\tilde{\mathsf{g}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{0}=\mathsf{g}
,
H=B
,
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{b}
,
\mathsf{x}=1_{\mathcal{A}}
, and

\mathsf{z}={-}\mathsf{a}\otimes I-\Theta\otimes\Xi+z\,1_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes I.

This gives, uniformly in
\alpha\neq\beta
and

\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
with
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2},\|\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq\upsilon
,

(110)

\begin{gathered}(1\otimes n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr})[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}]-(1\otimes\tau)[\mathsf{g}]\prec n^{-1},\\
\tilde{\mathsf{g}}_{\alpha\alpha}-(1\otimes\tau^{\mathcal{D}})[\mathsf{g}]_{\alpha\alpha}\prec n^{-1/2},\qquad\tilde{\mathsf{g}}_{\alpha\beta}\prec n^{-1/2},\\
(1\otimes\mathbf{v})^{*}\tilde{\mathsf{g}}(1\otimes\mathbf{v}^{\prime})-(1\otimes\mathbf{v})^{*}(1\otimes\tau^{\mathcal{D}})[\mathsf{g}](1\otimes\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\prec n^{-1/2}.\end{gathered}

We remark that if
T:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
and
T^{\prime}:\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
are two linear maps
between vector spaces, then for any
x\in\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
,

(111)

(T\otimes T^{\prime})[x]=(T\otimes 1)(1\otimes T^{\prime})[x]=(1\otimes T^{\prime})(T\otimes 1)[x].

Thus we may
combine the first statements of (
109
) and (
110
) to get
as desired

\displaystyle m(z)

\displaystyle=(n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\,\otimes\,n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr})[G]=n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\big[(n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\otimes I)[G]\big]

\displaystyle=(\tau\otimes n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr})[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}]+n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}[\Delta_{a}]=\tau\big[(1\otimes n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr})[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}]\big]+n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}[\Delta_{a}]

\displaystyle=\tau\otimes\tau[\mathsf{g}]+n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}[\Delta_{a}]+\tau[\Delta_{b}]=m_{0}(z)+n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}[\Delta_{a}]+\tau[\Delta_{b}]

where
\Delta_{a}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
and
\Delta_{b}\in\mathcal{A}
are errors satisfying

\Delta_{a},\Delta_{b}\prec n^{-1}
. We have

|n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Delta_{a}|\leq\|\Delta_{a}\|_{1}\prec n^{-1}

and
|\tau[\Delta_{b}]|\leq\|\Delta_{b}\|_{1}\prec n^{-1}
(Lemma
D.2
), showing (
21
). Similarly,
applying
(
111
) with
(T,T^{\prime})=(\mathbf{u}^{*}[\cdot]\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{*}[\cdot]\mathbf{v}^{\prime})

and
(T,T^{\prime})=(\tau^{\mathcal{D}},\mathbf{v}^{*}[\cdot]\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
,
we may combine the last
statements of (
109
) and (
110
) to get

\displaystyle(\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{v})^{*}G(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbf{v}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{v}^{*}\big[(\mathbf{u}\otimes I)^{*}G(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\otimes I)\big]\mathbf{v}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{v})^{*}(\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes I)[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}](\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbf{v}^{\prime})+\mathbf{v}^{*}\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{v}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{u}^{*}\tau^{\mathcal{D}}[(1\otimes\mathbf{v})^{*}\tilde{\mathsf{g}}(1\otimes\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]\mathbf{u}^{\prime}+\mathbf{v}^{*}\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{v}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{v})^{*}(\tau^{\mathcal{D}}\otimes\tau^{\mathcal{D}})[\mathsf{g}](\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbf{v}^{\prime})+\mathbf{v}^{*}\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{v}^{\prime}+\mathbf{u}^{*}\tau^{\mathcal{D}}[\Delta_{2}^{\prime}]\mathbf{u}^{\prime}

where
\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
and
\Delta_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
satisfy

\Delta_{1}^{\prime},\Delta_{2}^{\prime}\prec n^{-1/2}
. Since
\tau^{\mathcal{D}}
is a
L^{p}
-contraction
for each fixed
p
(Lemma
D.3
), this implies also

\tau^{\mathcal{D}}[\Delta_{2}^{\prime}]\prec n^{-1/2}
. Then, since

\Delta_{1}^{\prime},\tau^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
,
by Remark
3.3
we have

\|\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\tau^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta_{2}^{\prime})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec n^{-1/2}
and hence

\mathbf{v}^{*}\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\prec n^{-1/2}
and
\mathbf{u}^{*}\tau^{\mathcal{D}}[\Delta_{2}^{\prime}]\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\prec n^{-1/2}
,
showing (
28
). Finally, specializing this to

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{i}
and
\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
shows (
25
).
∎

In the remainder of this section, we show the final estimate (
27
) of Theorem
2.4
,

G_{ij,\alpha\beta}\prec n^{-1}

when
i\neq j
and
\alpha\neq\beta
. Recalling
\tilde{\mathsf{g}}
from
(
108
), define the matrices in
\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}

(112)

M_{B}=(\tau\otimes I)[\tilde{\mathsf{g}}],\qquad M_{i}=(B+\theta_{i}\Xi-zI-M_{B})^{-1}\text{ for }i=1,\ldots,n

so that (by Lemma
4.3
(b))

M_{B}=n^{-1}\sum_{i}M_{i}
. Symmetrically, letting

\check{\mathsf{g}}=(A\otimes 1_{\mathcal{A}}+I\otimes\mathsf{b}+\Theta\otimes\Xi-z\,I\otimes 1_{\mathcal{A}})^{-1}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}\otimes\mathcal{A},

define

(113)

M_{A}=(I\otimes\tau)[\check{\mathsf{g}}],\qquad M_{\alpha}=(A+\xi_{\alpha}\Theta-zI-M_{A})^{-1}\text{ for }\alpha=1,\ldots,n

so that
M_{A}=n^{-1}\sum_{\alpha}M_{\alpha}
.
We denote the commuting projection operators

\mathbb{E}_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\cdot|A^{(i)},B],\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{i}=1-\mathbb{E}_{i},\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{E}[\cdot|A,B^{[\alpha]}],\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha}=1-\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}

and write as before
\mathcal{Q}_{S}=\prod_{i\in S}\mathcal{Q}_{i}
.
With slight abuse of notation, we will use the distinction between Greek and
Roman indices to distinguish between
M_{\alpha}
and
M_{i}
,
\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}
and

\mathbb{E}_{i}
, and
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{i}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

In the setting of Theorem
2.4
, for any
distinct
i,j,k
and any
\alpha\neq\beta
, define

\Delta^{k}=G_{kj}E_{\beta\alpha}G_{ik}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}.

Fix any
l\geq 1
. Then uniformly over

S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
|S|\leq l
,
distinct
i,j,k\notin S
, and
\alpha\neq\beta
,

\mathcal{Q}_{S\cup\{k\}}[\Delta^{k}]\prec n^{-1-|S|/2}.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of Lemmas
4.12

and
4.13
, using Lemma
3.7
(c)
to expand
G_{kj},G_{ik}
in the indices of
S
and applying
the estimates
\|E_{\beta\alpha}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
and

G_{pq}^{(S^{\prime})}\prec n^{-1/2}
for
and
p,q\notin S^{\prime}
with
p\neq q
. We omit the details for brevity.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.4
, (
27
).

Throughout this proof,
G_{ij},G_{\alpha\beta}
etc. are all matrices in

\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
, so the stochastic domination notation
\prec
may be
understood in the operator norm sense,
c.f. Remark
3.3
.
We recall our convention of Roman indices
i,j,k,\ldots
for the first tensor
factor and Greek indices
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\ldots
for the second tensor
factor. In the following, we use a superscript
(\cdot)
to denote a minor
on
A
and
[\cdot]
to denote a minor on
B
.

Fix any indices
i\neq j
and
\alpha\neq\beta
. Applying Lemma

3.7
(b) to the second tensor factor,

G_{\alpha\beta}={-}G_{\alpha\alpha}\sum_{\gamma}^{[\alpha]}b_{\alpha\gamma}G_{\gamma\beta}^{[\alpha]}.
